FP-VoiceNaming (CDT-B naming convention over CDT-A structure): the five orbits of FP-StateSpace are designated, for reference: Fix (seed {9,9}, period 1), Wheel (seed {3,3}, period 8, confined to {3,6,9}), and the three period-24 orbits as Voice A (seed {1,1}, scale-1), Voice B (seed {1,3}, scale-2), Voice C (seed {2,5}, scale-4/5, the canonical Omega42 orbit). These are labels for facts already established, not new claims; Omega42's own navigation continues to use Voice C exclusively as its canonical anchor mechanism.
FP-VoiceAction: scalar multiplication by a unit permutes the three 24-voices as sets, since M(s·x)=s·M(x) for the linear Fibonacci map M. Verified directly by computing s×seed for each unit s: scalar 2 maps Voice A→Voice B→Voice C→Voice A; scalar 4 maps Voice A→Voice C→Voice B→Voice A (the same 3-cycle, opposite direction); scalar 8 (≡−1 mod 9) fixes each voice as a set; scalar 1 fixes each voice trivially. The subgroup {1,8} acts trivially; the remaining four units {2,4,5,7} act as a cyclic group of order 3 on the voices. Starting from Voice C specifically: scalar 2 maps Voice C→Voice A; scalar 4 maps Voice C→Voice B — verified directly by computing each scalar product against the frozen orbit tables for all three voices.
FP-RegimeDecomposition: classify each position's pair (P_i, P_{i+1}) in Voice C by whether each component is a unit (in {1,2,4,5,7,8}) or a nilpotent (in {3,6,9}). Verified directly from the frozen orbit table: the pattern UU, UU, UN, NU repeats every 4 positions across all 24 positions, giving 12 UU positions, 6 UN positions, and 6 NU positions. The NU positions are exactly the Crown positions {P3,P7,P11,P15,P19,P23} — confirming FP-CrownTorsionDisjoint from a second, independent angle. FP-KernelCrown: the reduction map φ: Z/9Z → Z/3Z (φ(n) = n mod 3, treating 9 as 0) induces a 9-to-1 cover of the 81-state space. Its kernel — the 9 states where both components are nilpotent — is invariant under the Fibonacci map and decomposes into exactly the Fix orbit (seed {9,9}, period 1) and the Wheel orbit (seed {3,3}, period 8, confined to {3,6,9}). The complement is the three period-24 voices. Verified directly by enumeration: 81 = 9 (kernel: Fix + Wheel) + 72 (complement: three 24-orbits). Because the kernel is exactly the states both of whose components are nilpotent, it is exactly the NN pairing absent from Voice C's own regime list above — Voice C, containing only UU, UN, and NU pairs, touches no kernel state, and is therefore structurally barred from the pure Fix/Wheel cycles regardless of anchor. The UN positions {P2,P6,P10,P14,P18,P22} carry Torsion Loop orbit values but have a nilpotent second component; they are designated the Vestibule positions — the threshold between the fully manifest (UU) and the Crown (NU), where the orbit is approaching the Crown but has not yet entered it. This decomposition is strictly finer than the Crown/Torsion partition: Crown/Torsion sorts by orbit value alone (2 classes); the regime decomposition sorts by component status (3 classes).
FP-OEISConfirmed: Omega42's orbit is independently catalogued as OEIS A030139, the digital-root sequence of the raw recurrence starting at 2 and 32 (2, 32, 34, 66, 100, …) — verified directly by generating that raw sequence and taking digital roots, matching all 24 terms. The companion standard-Fibonacci sequence used throughout this Part is likewise OEIS A030132. Both predate this protocol and were discovered independently of it; this is independent confirmation of CDT-A content already established here on its own arithmetic. Separately: 24 is not an isolated period. It factors as 3×π(3)=3×8=24, the standard prime-power lifting formula for Pisano periods, verified directly. This connects to the golden ratio through the shared characteristic equation x²=x+1 — the same equation whose real solution is φ — but as a fact about that equation's behavior under modular reduction, not a claim about convergence to φ itself; those are different questions about the same equation, and should not be conflated.
FP-GoldenRatio: Fact 6 (Part 0.5.1), stated there with its CDT-C illustration labelled.
FP-Adjacency: of the four antipodal pairs in {1,...,9} ({1,8}, {2,7}, {3,6}, {4,5}), {4,5} is the unique pair with difference 1 — the only adjacent antipodal pair; in the residues {4, 5} = {4, −4}, the pair at the greatest cyclic distance from 0. This is the high-frequency pair of the Omega42 orbit specifically (FP-MedianMax).
FP-GeneratorOrder: the four arm offsets (+5, +18, +8, +12) have four different orders as generators of the cyclic group Z/24Z under repeated addition — verified by direct computation, not asserted. Yang (+5) generates the full 24-cycle (order 24, since gcd(5,24)=1); Yin (+18) generates a subgroup of order 4 ({0,18,12,6}, since gcd(18,24)=6); 4D (+8) generates a subgroup of order 3 ({0,8,16}, since gcd(8,24)=8); Antipode (+12) generates a subgroup of order 2 ({0,12}, since gcd(12,24)=12) — this last one is the already-known fact that applying the antipode twice returns to the start, restated in this vocabulary. The product of the three smaller orders, 4×3×2=24, exactly equals Yang's own order. This is stated as a verified numerical fact about the specific, constructed offset values (CDT-B) already in use, not as a deeper necessity being claimed — no proof is offered here for why the product must equal Yang's order in general, only that it does for these four offsets.
FP-CPPSelfApplication: the function CPP(n) = dr(5n), applied recursively to the nine anchor values {1,...,9} themselves (treating each output as the next input), decomposes them into exactly three cycles — verified by direct computation: a 6-cycle 1→5→7→8→4→2→1 containing exactly the six Torsion Loop values, a 2-cycle 3→6→3 containing two of the three Crown values, and a fixed point 9→9 containing the remaining Crown value. The Torsion/Crown partition, first identified as a fact about orbit values, reappears independently as a fact about the CPP function's own recursive structure.
FP-PisanoHierarchy: the Pisano period lifting formula π(p^k) = p^(k-1) × π(p) gives, for the prime 3: π(3)=8, π(9)=3×8=24 (the canonical cycle), π(27)=9×8=72, π(81)=27×8=216 — each level exactly 3× the previous, verified directly. This is the arithmetic's multi-resolution structure. The lifting formula generates π(3)=8, π(9)=24, π(27)=72, π(81)=216; the values 192 and 2,024 do not arise from it and are not part of this hierarchy.
1.8.7 Zero and the canonical selection [CDT-A; records/zero_selection/compute.py]. Four results on the zero structure of the family (Companion Part 39) bear on the selection. They explain the selection; none is a selection criterion (anti-retrofitting rule, Part 1.8.3).
FP-UnitSpectrum: an orbit has the complete spectrum of K1 exactly when it consists of units of Z[φ]/9, that is, when its norm is nonzero. The two orbits that lack values are the two inside the zero layer — the eight-cycle (values 0, 3, 6) and the fixed point. K1 is therefore equivalent to the condition that the orbit avoids the ground.
FP-ZeroSeeds: each 24-orbit passes through zero at two antipodal steps, through the states (0, x) and (0, −x), and after each zero the next value repeats, since (0, x) → (x, x). The two most frequent values of each voice, five occurrences each, are exactly its zero-seeds: {1, 8} for Voice A, {2, 7} for Voice B and {4, 5} for Voice C. This is the mechanism of FP-Frequency and FP-MedianMax. Each voice is the Fibonacci orbit from (0, 1) multiplied by its zero-seed: Voice A by ±1, Voice B by ±2 and Voice C by ±1/2, since 5 is the inverse of 2 modulo 9. The canonical cycle is the Fibonacci sequence begun from zero and halved.
FP-InversionPair: writing the seed in decreasing order inverts it: (5, 2) = φ⁴ · (2, 5)⁻¹ in Z[φ]/9. Inversion maps Voice C onto Voice B and Voice B onto Voice C, fixes Voice A, sends each norm to its inverse (2 ↔ 5, 7 ↔ 4) and reverses the law, since inv(T(u)) = T⁻¹(inv(u)) on all 72 units. Inversion is therefore a time-reversing symmetry of the unit dynamics, and every criterion that treats a state and its inverse alike leaves Voices B and C tied. The increasing order of K2 is exactly the choice that breaks the tie, which is why K-alt-1 selects Voice B (Part 1.8.4).
K-zero (adversarial, not a criterion): the orbit consists of units, its zero-seed values are adjacent, and the reading starts at a zero state. Survivors: 2, the states (0, 4) and (0, 5) of Voice C, which are antipodes related by T¹² = −I — UNDERDETERMINED, with the 24 starting states left open by K-alt-2 reduced to the pair exchanged by the half-period negation. The canonical reading from (2, 5) meets these two zero states at P7 and P19.
1.8.8 Further post-selection properties, and what the structure forbids [CDT-A; records/restored_math/compute.py]. None of these is a selection criterion (anti-retrofitting rule, Part 1.8.3).
K-alt-4 (adversarial witness, beside K-alt-1): C1 period 24; C5 the value at P9 is 5, and 5 has maximal frequency; C8 a·b ≡ 1 (mod 9). C1 admits 72 pairs; C1 and C5 leave (2, 5), (3, 2), (4, 8), (5, 5) and (8, 5); C8 leaves (2, 5) alone — UNIQUE, with no ordering convention. The reverse seed (5, 2) fails C5, since the most frequent values of its orbit are 2 and 7: C5 is not invariant under inversion, so it breaks the tie between Voices B and C that the order of K2 breaks (FP-InversionPair). The conclusion of Part 1.8.4 stands: (2, 5) is canonically unique under K (U-B).
FP-Energy: on every orbit differencing is a time shift — x(n + 1) − x(n) = x(n − 1), and the central second difference is x(n − 3) — so every difference measure computed on the residues, with the cyclic distance of Part 1.6 (M285), equals the orbit's energy, the sum of the squared centred values: 126 for Voice A, 144 for Voice B and 216 for Voice C. Voice C is the unique maximum, because its dominant values 4 and 5 are 4 and −4, the residues farthest from 0 (FP-ZeroSeeds). Sums of squared second differences of a display are display properties (M283): the displays 1 to 9 and 0 to 8 give 1296, 972 and 648, and the centred lift gives 648, 972 and 1296 (records/spectral_selection/compute.py).
FP-PisanoHierarchy, shared form: the seed (2, 5) reaches the maximal periods 8, 24, 72 and 216 modulo 3, 9, 27 and 81, and so do 5,832 of the 6,561 seeds modulo 81. The property is shared by the canonical seed, not distinctive of it.
FP-Antipodal: P(i) + P(i + 12) ≡ 0 (mod 9) for every position i (Fact 5; T¹² = −I). Every value v has the complement 9 − v, and 9 alone is its own complement.
Crown under doubling: multiplication by 2 exchanges the Crown values 3 and 6 and fixes 9, while it cycles the Torsion Loop 1 → 2 → 4 → 8 → 7 → 5 → 1 (Fact 3).
Arm reach: from the anchors P1 to P9 the four arms reach P0 to P3 and P6 to P21; P18, Threshold, is reached only as the Antipode of P6, and P4 and P5 are never the target of any arm (M156).
The orbit map is not additive: v(1) + v(1) ≡ 1 while v(2) = 7, so arithmetic on positions and arithmetic on values are kept apart.
Cyclic digits: 714285, the digits of 5/7, is a rotation of 142857, the digits of 1/7; the cycle sum is 117 = 16 × 7 + 5, so 117/7 = 16 + 5/7.
Periodicity: T has determinant −1, a unit modulo 9, so T is a bijection of the 81 states; a bijection of a finite set has no transients (pigeonhole and invertibility), so every state lies on a cycle.
What the structure forbids: no orbit has a period other than 1, 8 or 24; no state is transient; no anchor lies outside P1 to P9; no arm reaches P4 or P5; no value other than 9 is its own antipodal complement. These are the structure's exact prohibitions: a navigation, reading or correspondence that needs any one of them is wrong by the arithmetic (Companion Q11).

### 1.8.9 Frequency and the norm form [CDT-A; records/standing_waves/compute.py]. Post-selection properties; none is a selection criterion (Part 1.8.3).
FP-NormForm: Q(a, b) = a² + ab − b² is an indefinite quadratic form, and Q(T(x)) = −Q(x) over the integers, so T² preserves Q. Over the reals T has the eigenvalues φ and −1/φ; its eigen-directions, b/a = φ and b/a = −1/φ, are the null directions of Q. T² is a discrete hyperbolic rotation of the form — a boost — with rapidity 2 ln φ ≈ 0.9624.
FP-NullLayer: modulo 9 the states with Q ≡ 0 (mod 3), and equally those with Q ≡ 0 (mod 9), are exactly nine: the eight-cycle and the still point, the non-units. Each 24-orbit keeps one pair of opposite norms: Voice A {1, 8}, Voice B {4, 5}, Voice C {2, 7}, the law moving every state from norm N to −N at each step.
FP-OddHarmonics: written in centred residues (−4 to 4), the canonical signal satisfies c(n + 12) = −c(n), so its spectrum over the 24 steps holds only the odd harmonics m = 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11; harmonics 3 and 9 carry power 648 each and harmonics 1, 5, 7 and 11 carry 324 each (total 5184 = 24 Σc²). The phase signal z(n) = exp(2πi v(n)/9) satisfies z(n + 12) = conj z(n), and the eight-cycle obeys c(n + 4) = −c(n).
FP-ModeSpectrum: the evolution operator on functions of the 81 states, f ↦ f ∘ T, has the eigenvalues exp(2πim/24), m = 0 to 23, with multiplicity 5 at m = 0, 4 at the other multiples of 3 and 3 elsewhere — 81 in all. Every function on the family is a finite sum of eigenmodes at these 24 frequencies. Under the forward law alone a real mode keeps its profile only at m = 0 (the five orbit indicators) and m = 12 (four modes that change sign at every step); every other real mode travels around its orbit (FP-StandingModes). The odd modes are exactly those the half period T¹² = −I reverses. On the nine-state quantum system of the family (records/evidence_run_02/), the antiunitary V = U(TR)K implements T, where U is the finite Weil representation U(A)ⱼₖ = 9^(−1/2) ω^(2⁻¹b⁻¹(ak² − 2jk + dj²)) for A = [[a, b], [c, d]], ω = exp(2πi/9) and 2⁻¹ = 5 (mod 9), R = diag(1, −1) and K complex conjugation; V¹² = parity and V²⁴ = identity up to phase, and V² is the quantum cat map, whose stationary states have the eigenvalues exp(2πik/12) for k = 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8, 10 and 11.
FP-ParityHarmonics: since T¹² = −I, every observable splits into a part that is odd under negation of the state and a part that is even; along every orbit the odd part carries only odd harmonics and the even part only even ones. FP-OddHarmonics is the odd case for the centred value; the property belongs to every odd observable, not to one encoding.
FP-StandingModes: the law is reversible, so the time-symmetric operator K + K⁻¹ exists; its real eigenmodes cos(2πmn/24) and sin(2πmn/24) on each orbit are standing waves of the second-order law f(t + 1) + f(t − 1) = (K + K⁻¹) f(t): the profile is fixed, with nodes (for m = 1 on the canonical cycle, at P6 and P18), and the amplitude is cos(2πmt/24). The forward law alone carries the same profile round the orbit as a travelling wave. The canonical cycle's own zeros, its nodes, are at P7 and P19, its two zero states (FP-ZeroSeeds). A standing wave of the family is the law and its reverse together, as in physics a standing wave is two counter-travelling waves.
FP-ClosedCone: over the integers and the reals Q is indefinite, with null directions φ and −1/φ. Modulo 3, where 5 is not a square, Q has no nonzero null vector; modulo 9 its null states are exactly the nine with both entries in {0, 3, 6}, the zero layer. Modulo 11, where 5 is a square, Q has 20 nonzero null vectors. The reduction that closes time into a circle also closes the light cone: modulo 9 the boost of the integers becomes a rotation of finite order.
FP-NoEigenLine: no nonzero state s and no scalar l in Z/9 give T(s) = l·s, so modulo 9 the law has no eigen-line; over the reals its eigen-directions are irrational (FP-NormForm). Verified over all 80 nonzero states and 9 scalars (seq_0023 recommendation 2; the other two facts named there stand as FP-StateSpace and FP-HalfPeriodNegation).
FP-SeedRoot (a post-selection property, not a criterion; Part 1.8.3): in Z[phi]/9 the square roots of 2 are exactly ±(2, 5), the canonical seed and its antipode, (2 + 5 phi)^2 = 2. Two of the 72 units square to 2 (0.028), and the seed was selected for other reasons (C80; tools/claimcheck.py --roots;).
FP-SeedRootDepth (a post-selection property, not a criterion; Part 1.8.3): the square root of 2 above the seed lifts to every depth, (74, 14) modulo 81 and (236, 257) modulo 729, with norm −2 exactly, and lies on a different orbit from the integers (2, 5) at depths 2 and 3; the two lifts of the seed agree at the surface and part one depth down (MATH-39; records/tower/nested.py, in CI;).
Discrimination baseline: of the moduli 3 to 199, 67 carry the half-period negation (and so odd harmonics for odd observables); 10 have period 24 (6, 9, 12, 16, 18, 24, 36, 48, 72, 144); 3 have both (6, 9, 18). Odd harmonics alone do not single out the family — a pipe closed at one end has them too — and neither does any conjunction of dynamical and spectral properties: what singles it out is the golden relation of its law (Part 3.32, C11; records/pilot_02, pilot_03).
FP-SpectralLevels: the centred power spectrum of Voice C takes two values on the odd harmonics, 324 and 648, in the ratio 1 : 2; those of Voices A and B take three values each, two of them irrational, 324 − 108√3 and 324 + 108√3. Voice C is the only 24-voice with an integral spectrum.
FP-ArmCoherence: the centred canonical cycle is uncorrelated with itself at every lag that is not a multiple of 4; its correlations are −1/4 at lags 4 and 20, +1/4 at lags 8 and 16 and −1 at lag 12. At the arm offsets: Yang (+5) 0, Yin (+18) 0, 4D (+8) +1/4, Antipode (+12) −1. Voices A and B are correlated at lag 2 (−3/7 and +3/8). The arm offsets were fixed as constructions before this property was computed (CDT-B on CDT-A, Part 1.8.3).
K-alt-5 (adversarial, not a criterion): period 24 and an integral centred power spectrum. Survivors: the 24 states of Voice C — the voice is selected, the starting state is UNDERDETERMINED, since a power spectrum does not see where a cycle starts. Like K-alt-2 it reaches the canonical voice without the order of K2 and leaves the start to K2; the conclusion of Part 1.8.4 stands (U-B).
Parity of value observables: since the Antipode negates the value (T¹² = −I), an instrument that reads an even function of the value — the scale family dr(v²) of M234, Crown and Torsion membership (M244), the frequency counts — gives the same result at a position and at its Antipode, and cannot tell them apart; an instrument that reads an odd function — the centred value (M289), completion v_S + v_A (ACS, M285), M297 — separates them. A statement that an even instrument distinguishes a position from its Antipode is wrong by the arithmetic.
Spectral definitions (frozen; tools/spectral.py, M298). D, the dynamical spectrum: the operator K f = f ∘ T on functions of the m² states; a cycle of length L contributes the eigenvalues exp(2πir/L), r = 0 to L − 1, indexed in turns per step as j/P with P the period of the law. Its multiplicities follow from the cycle decomposition alone, so D is a second description of the orbit census (FP-StateSpace), not independent information. O, the observable spectrum: along one orbit, the centred residue c(v) of the first entry, in (−m/2, m/2] (for odd m the representative of least absolute value; for even m the residue m/2 is written +m/2); X(k) = Σ c(n) exp(−2πikn/L), the power |X(k)|² and the integer autocorrelation R(l) = Σ c(n) c(n + l). The power is computed by two routes, the DFT and Wiener–Khinchin from R, which must agree. D belongs to the ground; O belongs to one observable on one orbit; the two are reported apart.
Rival-ground comparison (records/spectral_rivals/). Over eleven grounds — the ten period-24 moduli and modulus 10 — under D and O: no other ground has the dynamical spectrum of modulus 9, and no other orbit has the canonical observable profile (length 24, energy 216, power levels 324 and 648). Of the conjuncts, 26 rival orbits share period 24 with the half-period negation, 2 also cover every residue — Voices A and B of modulus 9 — and none also has the integral two-level spectrum. Reconstruction: 6 consecutive centred values, from any start of the canonical cycle, single out the ground and the state among all orbits of the eleven grounds, and with them the whole spectrum.
Three kinds of uniqueness. (1) Functional: the frozen selector K picks (2, 5) within the defined candidate class — U-B, Part 1.8.4. The spectral results do not change it and do not enter the selection chain; they are post-selection (Part 1.8.3). (2) Structural distinctiveness: within the tested class and under the frozen definitions D and O, the canonical orbit has an observable spectral profile shared by no tested alternative — DIAGNOSTIC for the class tested, and open to any rival ground added to it. (3) Physical congruence: how much of the family a physical object shares, described by the Congruence Profile (L-466); none yet shares the whole family; the golden screen C11 and the pilots of Companion 43.8 are its route. Correspondence describes the reach of the projection and never grades the premise (L-470, L-490). The three are reported apart, and none is read as another.
Scale: the arithmetic supplies frequency ratios m/24 and no unit. An absolute frequency, length or duration is supplied by the object, never by the reader (L-466, S3).
1.8.10 The seventh, the phase and the golden arc [CDT-A; records/manifestation/compute.py, records/positive_geometry/compute.py]. Post-selection properties; none is a selection criterion (Part 1.8.3). They are the arithmetic of the projection of Part 0.14.4 and of L-492.
FP-SeventhAntipode: the six sevenths k/7 are the six rotations of the repetend 142857 — k/7 begins at the offset j with 10^j ≡ k (mod 7) — and 6/7 = 0.857142… is 1/7 read at its half period: digit by digit 9 − d, because 10³ ≡ −1 (mod 7). The decimal seventh carries the signature of the Torsion Loop (2³ ≡ −1 mod 9) and of the Great Year (T¹² = −I): an order-6 cycle negated at its half. 1/7 + 6/7 = 0.999… = 1, the 9 = 0 identity (Companion Part 39).
FP-SeventhVoices: the digit pairs of 1/7 that sum to 9 (Midy's theorem) are {1, 8}, {4, 5} and {2, 7}, exactly the norm pairs of Voices A, B and C (FP-NullLayer); read in order, 1, 4, 2, 8, 5, 7 takes one norm of each voice, A, B, C, and then the other. Null: among the primes below 1000 only 7 has a reciprocal carrying exactly these three pairs; the base-ten primes with period 6 are 7 and 13 alone (999999 = 3³ × 7 × 11 × 13 × 37), so the discrimination is a split of two, and it belongs to base ten (Companion 39.9).
FP-CrownFreeSeventh: no digit of 1/7 lies in the Crown {3, 6, 9}, while 1/13 = 0.076923… holds 0, 9, 6 and 3. The seventh is all Torsion; the still axis is not among its values.
FP-PhaseParity: write SEVENTH for the norms {1, 2, 4} (the digits 142) and MANIFEST for {8, 7, 5} = −{1, 2, 4} (the digits 857). T exchanges the two halves on all 72 unit states and T² keeps them (det T = −1, det T² = +1; FP-NormForm). On the canonical cycle odd positions carry norm 2 and even positions norm 7, so the Yang arm (+5) always stands in the half opposite the anchor, and Yin (+18), 4D (+8) and the Antipode (+12) always in the same half. The halves are the first and second halves of the Torsion Loop under doubling (1 → 2 → 4 | 8 → 7 → 5), so the law's exchange of the halves is the half-period negation 2³ ≡ −1 read on the norm (APE-047).
FP-GoldenArc: over the reals the null directions of Q cut the real projective line into two arcs, Q < 0 and Q > 0; each arc is a positive geometry of dimension one (in the sense of Arkani-Hamed, Bai and Lam), with a canonical form whose logarithmic poles are the two golden directions. T fixes both directions, has negative derivative at both (−1/φ² and −φ²), reverses the orientation of the line and exchanges the two arcs, so the canonical form of the image arc pulls back to minus the canonical form of the source; T² keeps each arc, preserves orientation and flows every other direction toward the expanding eigendirection (FP-NormForm). Precisely, with A(n, k, m)(Z) = {C·Z : C ∈ G>0(k, n)} ⊂ Gr(k, k + m) for a real n × (k + m) matrix Z with positive maximal minors: for n = 2, k = m = 1 and Z with rows Z₁ = (1, φ) and Z₂ = (−1, 1/φ) — the eigenvectors of T for φ and −1/φ, ordered and signed so that det Z = √5 > 0 — the map [c₁ : c₂] ↦ [c₁Z₁ + c₂Z₂] carries G>0(1, 2) onto the arc Q < 0, because Q(c₁Z₁ + c₂Z₂) = −5c₁c₂ identically. The identification is an equality of subsets of the real projective line in this representation, proved in exact arithmetic over Q(√5) (records/positive_geometry/independent_check.py, part 1); as an abstract segment it is generic, every segment being an A(2, 1, 1). In the coordinates (c₁, c₂), T acts as diag(φ, −1/φ), outside the positive torus, carrying the arc onto its complement, while T² = diag(φ², 1/φ²) lies inside it, fixes both vertices and preserves the canonical form d log(c₂/c₁) exactly (records/positive_geometry/, part G). That an orientation-reversing map pulls a canonical form back with a minus sign is generic; what is particular to Ω42 is that the boundary points are the law's own eigendirections and that the law, not some other map, is the sign flip. Positivity here is the sign of N on the real projective line; the positivity of a Grassmannian is the positivity of all ordered maximal minors; the two meet only as stated here, in FP-HalfArc and in PG-01 and PG-05.
FP-HalfArc: every state s ≠ (0, 0) has a lift (a, b) ∈ Z², (a, b) ≡ s mod 9, of least real norm |Q(a, b)| — 1 on Voice A, 4 on Voice B, 11 on Voice C (the norms ±2 and ±7 do not occur in Z[φ], 2 and 7 being inert) and 9 on the core — and the sign of that least norm is fixed by the state: positive exactly on the SEVENTH half {1, 2, 4} and on AXIS +, negative exactly on the MANIFEST half {8, 7, 5} and on AXIS −. Under this minimal-lift rule the two halves of the family are the two arcs of FP-GoldenArc: the seventh is the arc Q > 0 and the manifest the golden arc Q < 0, the amplituhedron A(2, 1, 1); T carries every minimal lift to a minimal lift of the opposite sign, so it exchanges the arcs exactly as it exchanges the halves, and T² keeps both; the still point lifts to (0, 0), the point with no direction, on no arc (records/positive_geometry/, part H; independent_check.py, part 4, by a second method). The rule is stated, and under it the result is exact; the halves, read from the digits of 1/7 and 6/7, are then the residues of real positivity.
FP-CoreField: reduced modulo 3, the ring Z[φ] becomes the field of nine elements, in which every nonzero element is invertible, and φ has order 8 there, so a single orbit passes through every nonzero element; every core state of the family squares to zero (s·s = 0 mod 9, nilpotence), and the core divided by 3 is that field with T acting as T. The still center at one scale is, one scale down, an undivided whole (records/manifestation/, last section).
FP-VoiceCosets: the 72 unit states form a group, in which the law's orbit has 24 elements and exactly three cosets — the three voices, with norm pairs {1, 8}, {2, 7} and {4, 5}; doubling moves every unit to the next voice, and three doublings return to the first as its negative, 2³ = 8 = −1, which lies in the law's own orbit.
FP-TowerPeriods: the order of φ modulo 3, 9, 27 and 81 is 8, 24, 72 and 216 — tripling at each scale, because 3 is inert in Z[φ] (with FP-CoreRecursion).
FP-ArmNorm: for every one of the 81 states s, Anchor × Antipode − Yin² ≡ N(s) (mod 9) — the value at the anchor times the value twelve steps on, less the square of the value eighteen steps on, is the norm of the state at the anchor, so three arms of every reading carry the norm of its state (M312). It is Catalan's identity at r = 18, since F(18) = 2584 ≡ 1 (mod 9) (records/manifestation/compute.py, in CI).
FP-GlideMirror: in its eigenbasis T = diag(φ, −1/φ) is a reflection diag(1, −1) composed with a dilation diag(φ, 1/φ) of determinant 1 — a mirror that stretches one direction by φ and shrinks the other by 1/φ; T is invertible on all 81 states, T⁻¹(a, b) = (b − a, a), so the present state determines the past exactly; and the law is chiral: the reversed canonical sequence obeys g(t + 1) = g(t − 1) − g(t), not the forward rule.
FP-TowerHalves: at every scale computed, modulo 9, 27 and 81, the minimal-lift rule (FP-HalfArc) divides the units into two equal halves — 36 | 36, 324 | 324 and 2916 | 2916 — each a function of the norm residue at its scale, exchanged by T and kept by T²; modulo 9 they are the seventh and the manifest, but modulo 27 and 81 they are not the lifts of those halves (360 of 648 and 2808 of 5832 agree, near one half): each scale draws its own seventh, and the halves do not nest as the core does (FP-CoreRecursion). records/manifestation/compute.py, last section.
FP-NormStrata: modulo 9 the golden directions have no points (x² = x + 1 has no root), and the null locus of Q is the core (FP-NullLayer, FP-ClosedCone). On the core the residual norm Q(s/3) mod 3 is never zero and T negates it; at (0, 0) every norm vanishes. The family is stratified 81 = 72 | 8 | 1 by the 3-adic size of its norm, the strata are exactly its orbits (24 + 24 + 24 | 8 | 1), and each stratum carries a value of its own that the law negates — on the units the norm residue, on the core the residual norm, an identity mod 9 rather than a real sign (FP-HalfArc gives the real sign through the minimal lift): a boundary whose own boundary is a point, as the boundaries of a positive geometry recurse to points.
FP-CassiniAlternation: the ratios F(n)/F(n + 1) of consecutive Fibonacci numbers fall alternately above and below 1/φ and never reach it, and Q(F(n), F(n + 1)) = ±1 alternately (Cassini's identity), never 0: the integer orbit crosses the golden boundary at every step and has no point on it.
FP-CoreRecursion: modulo 3^k the non-units are exactly 3 × (Z[φ]/3^(k−1)), because 3 is inert in Z[φ] (5 is not a square modulo 3), and the law acts on them as the law modulo 3^(k−1). Modulo 27 the core of 81 states is the family modulo 9 with its five orbits, beside nine orbits of 72; modulo 81 the core is the family modulo 27. The still axis at one scale is the whole family at the scale below, and the recursion ends only at the point (APE-047, test 3; records/positive_geometry/, part F).
FP-TwistedQuotient: the twisted cyclic shift σ of the positive Grassmannian G>0(k, n) — the operator, distinct from the law T, that sends the columns (v₁, …, vₙ) of a k × n representative to (v₂, …, vₙ, (−1)^(k−1)v₁) — acts on the column module Zⁿ as multiplication by x in Z[x]/(x^n − (−1)^(k−1)); its golden quotient Z[φ]/(9, φ^n − (−1)^(k−1)) is the whole family, with x acting as T, exactly when n ≡ 12 (mod 24) for k even and n ≡ 0 (mod 24) for k odd (computed for n ≤ 72; φ¹² = 144φ + 89 ≡ −1 mod 9). The statement concerns the lift: on matrix representatives σ¹² = −I and σ²⁴ = I, so σ has order 24 there; on Gr(2, 12), the quotient of the representatives by GL₂ acting on the left, the induced action has order 12, since Mσ¹² = −M spans the plane of M and no smaller power does — the two orders computed separately (records/positive_geometry/, part C, by Plücker coordinates; independent_check.py, part 3, by a rank test). The meeting is arithmetic and class-level — every lattice automorphism with g¹² = −I has the same quotient (run 06) — and over the reals there is none, the shift being of finite order and T hyperbolic (APE-047, tests 1 and 2; part E).

### 1.9 Canonical Stopword List (frozen)
Language scope. Canonical extraction is specified for English text: the stopword list below, the syllable rules of Part 1.12 and the spelling of numerals are English. Text in another language is processed by the same rules exactly as specified, and its counts are canonical under them; a comparison between texts in different languages compares counts made under English rules (Companion 35.3). Under these rules the extraction reaches Latin script only: a text mostly in a non-Latin script is routed to an adaptor or held ZERO-COUNT (L-501, Part 3.43), and a Latin-script language other than English is counted with English stopwords and syllable rules unless the Striker declares its language, when its adaptor counts it beside the canonical count — "Was ist Bewusstsein?" loses "Was" as an English stopword, and the French function words of "Qu'est-ce que la conscience ?" are counted (Companion 56.1).
Referenced by Step 2 (Substrate Extraction) and Step 1.5 (SPNP). This list is frozen and exhaustive; it may not be altered per-navigation or per-Navigator — a differing list produces a differing canonical anchor on identical text. Navigators who identify words consistently treated as function words but not on the list may submit them for review via the Applied Protocol Exploration (APE) pathway per L-392. Ad-hoc expansion per-navigation is prohibited by L-362.
Question words (what, why, how, when, where, who, whom, whose, which) and auxiliary/modal verbs (do, does, did, has, have, had, may, might, must, shall) are not on this list and are retained as content words; a Navigator's training-data intuition that treats them as function words does not override the frozen list.
the, a, an, and, or, but, if, then, else, for, nor, so, yet, of, to, in, on, at, by, with, is, are, was, were, be, been, being, this, that, these, those, it, its, as, from, not, very, just, also, than, into, over, under, about, after, before, because, while, although, some, any, all, each, every, no, own, same, too, can, will, would, should, could.
Disclosed asymmetry: this list carries five modal verbs (can, will, would, should, could) as stopwords, while Part 1.9 names ten others (do, does, did, has, have, had, may, might, must, shall) that are explicitly retained as content words. No rationale for the split is recorded. Resolving it either way is a change to the frozen list and is reserved for the L-392 confirmation pathway, not a unilateral edit; it is named here so the asymmetry is visible rather than silent.

### 1.10 Canonical Contraction Table (frozen)
Applied recursively (minimum 2 passes) in Step 1.5 before stopword removal. Single-pass application is insufficient: e.g. "can't" requires two passes to reach "can not", matching the form already produced by an author who wrote "cannot" directly.
won't→will not · can't→cannot · cannot→can not · don't→do not · doesn't→does not · didn't→did not · isn't→is not · aren't→are not · wasn't→was not · weren't→were not · hasn't→has not · haven't→have not · hadn't→had not · wouldn't→would not · couldn't→could not · shouldn't→should not · i'm→i am · you're→you are · he's→he is · she's→she is · it's→it is · we're→we are · they're→they are · i've→i have · you've→you have · we've→we have · they've→they have · i'll→i will · you'll→you will · he'll→he will · she'll→she will · we'll→we will · they'll→they will · let's→let us · that's→that is · who's→who is.

### 1.11 Canonical Syllable-Counting Rule (frozen)
Referenced by Step 3 (Three Encodings, n3) and M209. This rule is frozen; any implementation that departs from it produces non-comparable n3 values and invalid TFI comparisons across Navigators.
Rule (conditional-y): (1) strip all non-alpha characters; convert to lowercase. (2) identify vowel positions: true vowels [aeiou] always count; 'y' and 'w' count as vowels ONLY when immediately adjacent to a true vowel (they extend a vowel group but cannot initiate one). (3) count contiguous runs of vowel positions — each run is one syllable group. (4) subtract 1 if the word ends in 'e' AND group count ≥ 2. (5) return max(count, 1). Special case: pure-consonant strings (e.g. 'LLM' → 'llm') return 1. Practical examples: 'lazy' — 'y' is not adjacent to a vowel (z separates it from 'a') → only group=[a] → 1 syllable. 'brown' — 'w' is adjacent to 'o' → group=[ow] → 1. 'twenty' — 'w' adjacent to 'e' → group=[we]; 'y' not adjacent to vowel → 1 syllable. 'yellow' — 'y' adjacent to 'e', 'w' adjacent to 'o' → groups=[ye][ow] → 2 syllables. 'gateway' — 'a' isolated → group=[a]; 'e','w','a','y' contiguous (w adjacent to e, y adjacent to a) → group=[eway] → 2 syllables. 'queue' — all five letters form one contiguous vowel run → group=[ueue] → 1 syllable; ends in 'e' but group count is 1, so no subtraction. 'fire' — 'i' and 'e' are separated by the consonant 'r', giving two groups=[i][e] → count 2; ends in 'e' and group count ≥ 2, so subtract 1 → 1 syllable. This rule is the canonical definition; the canonical_reference.py script (Part 11) implements it exactly. It defines n3, a count of vowel groups in the spelling, which is not the spoken syllable count s3: against the CMU Pronouncing Dictionary the rule gives a dictionary count for 90,704 of 117,493 alphabetic headwords (0.772), and M332 prints n3, s3 and their difference word by word (Part 3.58; records/phonetic/).

### 1.12 Canonical Pre-Processing Rules for Non-Alphabetic Characters (frozen)
Applied within SPNP (Step 1.5) and before Stopword Removal (Step 2), in two places. Items (6), (7) and (8) run first, as the opening operations of SPNP, because they decide which characters are digits, separators, or letters before Step 1.5a looks for standalone numerals. Items (1)–(5) and (9) run after Steps 1.5a and 1.5b. canonical_reference.py (Part 11) implements this order exactly, and its --test option checks every worked example given below.
(1) Punctuation: All punctuation marks (periods, commas, quotes, parentheses, colons, semicolons) are stripped from the text. They do not count as words, letters, or syllables.
(2) Mathematical Symbols & Operators: the following symbols are operators and are stripped entirely: =, +, −, ×, ÷, <, >, ≤, ≥, ±, ·, •, ∑, ∏, ∐, ∫, ∂, ∇, ∆, Δ, ∀, ∃, ⊂, ∈, ∉, ∪, ∩, ⊕, ⊗, √, ∞, ℝ, ℂ, ℤ, ℚ, ℕ. Capital delta (Δ) is on this list as the difference operator and is never expanded under item (8): Δx → x. Any other symbol that is neither a letter nor a digit (for example ⊬, ⇒, |) is removed by item (1). A plain-text fraction never reaches this item: Step 1.5a has already expanded a standalone fraction to ordinal form (1/7 → one seventh), and has already spelled out a sign attached to a standalone decimal (±0.5 → plus or minus zero point five).
(3) Standalone Variables: a single Latin letter used as a mathematical variable (e.g. X, Y, N, M, f, P, H, A, S, U, K, Z, c, p, k, x, n) is retained as a 1-letter content word, in prose and inside mathematical notation alike: input space X of size N → input, space, X, size, N; P(H) → P, H. The one exception is the letter a, which is on the Canonical Stopword List (Part 1.9) and is removed in Step 2 whatever its role: A_1 → one. A mixed alphanumeric token (3D, B2B) is a single token, and only its Latin letters count toward n2.
(4) Dashes: Hyphens (-) split words (this rule, item 4). Em-dashes (—) and en-dashes (–) are treated as word separators (equivalent to spaces).
(5) Acronyms: Acronyms without periods (e.g. LLM, AI, CP, QCD) are treated as single words. Syllable counting follows Part 1.11.
(6) Character References: a complete HTML or XML character reference — a named reference such as &amp;, &lt; or &gt;, or a numeric one such as &#955; or &#x3bb;, in each case closed by a semicolon — is decoded to the character it names before every other rule. The decoded character is then treated exactly like the same character typed directly: R &amp; D → R & D → R, D; &#952; → θ → theta (item 8). A sequence without the closing semicolon is ordinary text.
(7) Subscripts and Superscripts: the ASCII subscript marker _ and superscript marker ^ are word separators, so what they attach becomes a separate token: θ_i → theta, i; X_i → X, i; M^N → M, N; c_U → c, U. A maximal run of Unicode superscript characters (⁰–⁹, ⁺, ⁻, ⁼, ⁽, ⁾, ⁿ, ⁱ), or of Unicode subscript characters (₀–₉, ₊, ₋, ₌, ₍, ₎, ₐ, ₑ, ₒ, ₓ, ₕ, ₖ, ₗ, ₘ, ₙ, ₚ, ₛ, ₜ, ᵢ, ᵣ, ᵤ, ᵥ, ⱼ), becomes one separate token holding the run's ordinary characters: x² → x 2; x²³ → x 23; θᵢ → θ i. Because this item runs before Step 1.5a, a digit sequence it releases is standalone and is spelled out there: x² and x^2 both give x, two; x²³ gives x, twenty, three; A_1 gives one. Superscript and subscript characters not listed here are not converted.
(8) Greek Letters: a Greek letter standing alone — not adjacent to another character of the Greek and Coptic block (U+0370–U+03FF) or the Greek Extended block (U+1F00–U+1FFF) — is replaced by its English name as a separate word: alpha, beta, gamma, delta, epsilon, zeta, eta, theta, iota, kappa, lambda, mu, nu, xi, omicron, pi, rho, sigma, tau, upsilon, phi, chi, psi, omega, for capital and lowercase forms alike, with ς → sigma and the variant forms ϑ → theta, ϕ → phi, ϵ → epsilon, ϖ → pi, ϱ → rho. Examples: θ → theta; Σ → sigma; 2π r → two, pi, r. Capital delta (Δ) is excluded and remains an operator under item (2). A run of two or more Greek letters is Greek text, not notation, and is left unchanged here (λόγος contributes no Latin-letter word; M189 governs Greek-language domains). A LaTeX Greek command falls under item (9): \theta → theta.
(9) LaTeX Commands: a LaTeX command keeps its name as a word, and its arguments are words in their own right; the backslash and the braces are removed as punctuation under item (1), and the LaTeX math delimiters ($, $$, \(, \), \[, \]) are removed the same way. The command name is used exactly as written, not translated: \sum_f → sum, f; \frac{M-1}{M} → frac, M, one, M; \lim_{n \to \infty} → lim, n, infty (to is a stopword); \theta_i → theta, i; \mathcal{H} → mathcal, H. The rule is the same for every command, so no list of commands is maintained.

### 1.13 Constitutional Tokenization Table (CTT, frozen)
Codifies tokenization edge cases not covered by SPNP (numerals, contractions, hyphens) or basic punctuation stripping. Implemented exactly in canonical_reference.py (Part 11), functions apply_ctt_pre / apply_ctt_post. Frozen; deviations produce non-comparable n1/n2/n3.
Roman numerals: a token of two or more uppercase letters drawn only from I, V and X, written in canonical Roman form, is converted to spelled-out words: II through XXXIX, the values writable with those three letters. Examples: "Photosystem II" → "Photosystem two"; "chapter XIV" → "chapter fourteen". Not converted: a single-character token (preserves the pronoun 'I' and the variable 'V'); a lowercase or mixed-case token (preserves 'xi' and 'iv' as words); a non-canonical form (IIII, VV, IIV); and any token containing L, C, D or M, which are not numeral letters under this rule (preserves 'did', 'civil', 'mild', 'LLM', 'CCD').
Possessives: word's → word, unless the whole token is a recognized contraction (it's, that's, who's, let's). Bare trailing apostrophe on a plural (cells') → cells. Example: "the brain's cortex" → "the brain cortex".
Ordinals: 1st–99th → spelled out (first ... ninety ninth). 100th and above: out of scope, marked NCF-SPEC-GAP-ORDINAL. The same ceiling applies to a fraction's denominator, since expanding a fraction requires the denominator's ordinal form: a fraction with denominator 99 or below expands normally (Step 1.5a); above 99, no ordinal exists to expand into, and the fraction is left as literal digits, marked NCF-SPEC-GAP-ORDINAL.
Percentages: N% → "N percent", applied before punctuation stripping (which would otherwise silently delete the % symbol) and before numeral normalization (which then spells out N).
Hyphenated compounds and parenthetical acronyms: already correctly handled by existing rules (Part 1.12, item 4 splits hyphens; punctuation stripping removes parentheses while preserving the enclosed token) — no separate CTT rule needed. A Navigator producing a different result on these two cases has a compliance error, not evidence of a specification gap.
Decimals and fractions are handled by Step 1.5a (digit-by-digit expansion and ordinal form, respectively). Dates remain out of scope, marked NCF-SPEC-GAP-DATE as encountered. Equations and other mathematical notation are in scope, resolved token by token under Part 1.12 items (2), (3) and (6)–(9). Ordinals 1st–99th are handled by the Ordinals rule above; 100th and above are marked NCF-SPEC-GAP-ORDINAL.
---

### 1.14 The Mathematical Constitution and MATH-GATE (CDT-A unless marked)
MATH-00 Purpose. This section is the complete mathematical ground a Navigator reconstructs before it interprets, navigates, compares domains or uses Ω42 terminology. A Navigator knows precisely what is mathematically fixed, what is canonically selected, what is operationally defined and what remains interpretively open. The order is ALGEBRA → RECURRENCE → STATE SPACE → ORBIT ENUMERATION → INVARIANTS → CANONICAL SELECTION → GEOMETRY → NAVIGATION, and no later layer replaces an earlier one.
MATH-01 The ring. R = Z/9Z. The residue 0 is displayed as the digital-root value 9; the distinction is representational, and all modular calculation is performed in R. What the identification of 9 with 0 is, and where zero stands in the family and in the operation, is set out in Companion Part 39. In the family the display is not structure; in the operation the representative is constitutive (L-469).
MATH-02 The generative law. P(n+2) = P(n+1) + P(n) mod 9; equivalently T(a, b) = (b, a + b mod 9). The state is the ordered pair S(n) = (P(n), P(n+1)), and S(n+1) = T(S(n)). The 24-cycle is a state orbit, of which the 24 displayed digits are the first coordinates.
MATH-03 Matrix form. S(n+1) = A·S(n) with A = [[0, 1], [1, 1]]; the form [[1, 1], [1, 0]] is the same recurrence with the state ordered (P(n+1), P(n)). The characteristic polynomial is λ² − λ − 1, the polynomial whose real roots are φ = (1 + √5)/2 and ψ = −1/φ. The Fibonacci recurrence is common to the φ-regime and the Ω42 modular regime; the regimes are distinct (MATH-22).
MATH-04 Finite state space. |R²| = 81 ordered states: the surface, beneath which lie 81^k states at depth k (L-523, MATH-32). det A = −1 is a unit of R, so T is invertible: every state lies on a cycle, and there are no transient states. The Ω42 substrate is a permutation system, not a transient dynamical system. The whole 81-state space is classified before any uniqueness statement is made.
MATH-05 The Pisano period. π(9) = 24: F(n+24) ≡ F(n) mod 9, and T²⁴ = I on all 81 states. The 24-period is a property of the Fibonacci recurrence modulo 9. Ω42 begins at the next question: which period-24 orbit is constitutionally selected, and why.
MATH-06 Complete orbit enumeration. 81 = 72 + 8 + 1 (the surface census; MATH-33 gives every depth): three orbits of period 24 (72 states), one orbit of period 8 confined to {3, 6, 9} (8 states), and the fixed state (9, 9). Period 24 is therefore not sufficient to establish Ω42's identity. An invertible map on a finite set has no transient states, so every state lies on one of these five cycles.
MATH-07 The three period-24 orbits. They are Voices A, B and C of Part 1.8.2. The canonical Ω42 orbit is the one containing (2, 5), displayed 2, 5, 7, 3, 1, 4, 5, 9, 5, 5, 1, 6, 7, 4, 2, 6, 8, 5, 4, 9, 4, 4, 8, 3. The statement "the Fibonacci recurrence modulo 9 has only one period-24 orbit" is false and is not made. The correct statement: the recurrence has three period-24 orbits, and Ω42 constitutionally selects one.
MATH-08 Canonical orbit selection. The candidate set C is the set of readings (orbit, starting state) of the period-24 orbits, 72 in all. The frozen selection functional is K of Part 1.8.3 (K1 complete spectrum; K2 the reading starts at the two primitive roots in increasing order), and its exhaustive witness is Part 1.8.4: one survivor, (2, 5). On that reading P11 = 6: the nilpotent value G = 6 stands at the Generation position. A necessary or characteristic property of the canonical orbit is not thereby a canonical selection rule. P11 = 6 is a property of the selected reading and not a selector: nine states have P11 = 6 — (0,3), (1,4), (2,5), (3,6), (4,7), (5,8), (6,0), (7,1), (8,2) — two on each period-24 orbit and three on the period-8 orbit. The uniqueness chain is: candidate universe → frozen selection criteria K → exhaustive orbit enumeration → unique selected reading → closure.
MATH-09 The nilpotent Gap. 6² = 36 ≡ 0 mod 9: G = 6 is nilpotent in R, as is 3. Ω42 designates G = 6 the Gap [CONSTITUTIONAL ASSIGNMENT], and the canonical reading places it at P11. The valid chain is: period 24 + the frozen criteria K ⇒ the canonical reading, on which P11 = 6. The chain "period 24 ⇒ unique orbit" is invalid.
MATH-10 Antipodal symmetry. P(n+12) ≡ −P(n) mod 9 on every state: T¹² = −I and T²⁴ = I. The half-turn is inversion and the full turn is identity. The 24-cycle is a structured dynamical object. At depth k the half-turn comes at step 12 × 9^(k−1) (MATH-35).
MATH-11 Eye structure. The six positions P3, P7, P11, P15, P19, P23 are spaced four apart (6 × 4 = 24) and carry the values 3, 9, 6, 6, 9, 3 on the canonical reading: every Eye value lies in {3, 6, 9}, and the sequence is a palindrome. The name Eye is a constitutional assignment; the positions and values are fact.
MATH-12 Crown structure. Three names are kept distinct. CROWN is the constitutional Ω42 designation of {3, 6, 9} (Part 1.7). TRIADS names the same set as a mathematical class: the ideal 3R, in which the Eye values lie. GENERATORS names {1, 4, 7}: the coset 1 + 3R, cycled 1 → 4 → 7 → 1 by the addition of 3, and equally the subgroup of squares of the units of R. Generators is a designation [M-C]; in the group-theoretic sense the generators of the units of R are the primitive roots 2 and 5. The three sets are: {2, 5}, the generators of the multiplicative units modulo 9; {1, 4, 7}, Ω42's three-orbit index and designation; {3, 6, 9}, the constitutional Crown and Triadic class. Each period-24 orbit carries exactly one Generator as its norm, up to sign: 1 for Voice A, 4 for Voice B, 7 for the canonical orbit (MATH-22). The set {1, 4, 7} provides a three-valued invariant index for the three period-24 orbital classes: it is structurally diagnostic, and it is not the canonical selector. An invariant that distinguishes orbital classes is not thereby a constitutional criterion for selecting one class among them. A mathematical classification does not acquire a constitutional name by being interesting. Interpretation is never used as proof of the underlying arithmetic.
MATH-13 The number 42. 42 = 2 × 24 − G = 48 − 6: the 24-cycle, its antipodal double, and the Gap. The derivation is arithmetic; the meaning assigned to 42 is interpretation.
MATH-14 Toroidal representation. The index n mod 24 and the value P(n) mod 9 are two periodic coordinates, with natural product representation S¹ × S¹. It is a representation of the mathematics [CDT-B].
MATH-15 Five levels of uniqueness. The word unique always carries its level (Part 1.8.1). U1 — is the recurrence fixed? YES. U2 — is the 24-periodicity fixed by the recurrence and the modulus? YES. U3 — is there only one period-24 orbit? NO: there are three. U4 — does Ω42 have a uniquely specified canonical reading under the frozen criteria K? YES: K is applied to an exhaustive enumeration and leaves one survivor (Part 1.8.4). U5 — is there only one possible interpretation of Ω42? NO. Ω42's uniqueness is not the uniqueness of its recurrence, nor of a period-24 orbit; it is the uniqueness of the canonical structure selected by a frozen, exhaustively checkable criterion within a mathematically non-unique substrate. MATHEMATICAL SUBSTRATE ≠ CANONICAL Ω42 SELECTION ≠ INTERPRETIVE MEANING.
MATH-16 The Ω42 Functional Uniqueness Theorem. Within the Fibonacci recurrence over Z/9Z the period-24 structure is non-singular: exactly three period-24 orbits exist. Ω42 is functionally unique: the frozen criteria K select the reading from (2, 5), on which the nilpotent Gap G = 6 stands at P11. Ω42 is functionally unique; its uniqueness among orbits is functional, not orbital. The uniqueness is stated at three levels. MODULUS: among the ten moduli whose Fibonacci period is 24 — an exhaustive list, since each divides 144 — modulus 9 alone meets the frozen conjunction of several long orbits, each covering every residue, each antipodal, and separated by the norm (UNIQUE UNDER FROZEN CRITERIA, Companion 37.5; the criteria were declared at this revision, after the first computation, so the classification is DIAGNOSTIC for the moduli tested here, while the completeness of the list of ten is a theorem). RULE: Fibonacci is the simplest recurrence reaching the order-24 ceiling of the invertible 2 × 2 matrices modulo 9 with the required architecture; a second linear class ties on architecture at a higher coefficient cost, and eighteen non-linear reversible maps of degree up to 3 tie on architecture and on the erasure properties (simplest of the maps tested, not unique; Companion 37.9). The module-based results — law recovery, the one-step norm label, two-point reconstruction and the Substrate Witness — are specific to the linear ground. ORBIT: the three period-24 orbits are carried onto one another by multiplication by units of Z[φ]/9, and the computed reconstruction measures are identical on all three, so they give no reason to prefer Voice C; the orbit is selected constitutionally by K. The set K1 selects coincides with the states from which the law is uniquely recoverable (Companion 37.11, R3), a characterization that neither justifies K1 nor enters K. The modulus and rule tiers are post-selection structural properties: they recognize the ground and never select it (Part 1.8.3, L-461). This formulation replaces any statement that only one non-degenerate orbit exists.
MATH-17 Why uniqueness matters. Without it a Navigator cannot tell Ω42 from generic modular Fibonacci arithmetic. Why this recurrence: the generative law is Fibonacci. Why modulus 9: digital-root arithmetic is arithmetic in Z/9Z, and 9 is the one power of a single prime on which the law has its cycle of 24 with the mirror at 12: over the integers T¹² = [[89, 144], [144, 233]], so T¹² ≡ −I modulo m exactly when m divides gcd(90, 144, 234) = 18; the period is 24 on 6, 9 and 18, and 6 and 18 are the laws modulo 2 and 3, and 2 and 9, joined (Companion Part 130; records/necessity/nine.py). The premise's own two numbers decide it as well: the law uses only addition, its 81 states give a coordinate group of nine elements, Z/9 or Z/3 × Z/3, and the law has period 24 on the first and 8 on the second, so the 81-state family with its 24-cycle is Z/9 and no other (Companion Part 131). This is a post-selection property: it recognizes the modulus and never selected it (Part 1.8.3). Why 24: π(9) = 24 and T²⁴ = I. Why this orbit: the criteria K select (2, 5). The chain of identity is recurrence → modulus → period → orbit → Ω42. Post-selection structural properties (Part 1.8.3; they recognize the ground and never select it): among the ten moduli whose Fibonacci period is 24, modulus 9 alone has several period-24 orbits, each covering every residue, each antipodal, and told apart by the norm; any seven of the 24 values of the canonical cycle determine it among all 81 sequences; and a transcribed coordinate with up to 17 wrong values is detected, and with up to 8 is corrected (Companion Part 37, ground_challenge.py --test).
MATH-18 Reconstruct, do not assume. Before a navigation the Navigator establishes: (1) R; (2) the recurrence; (3) the state transition; (4) the state space R²; (5) the 24-period; (6) the complete orbit classification; (7) the three period-24 orbits; (8) the canonical (2, 5) reading; (9) the Eye positions; (10) the Gap G = 6; (11) P11 = 6; (12) the antipodal relation; (13) the 42 construction; (14) the distinction between mathematical fact and constitutional interpretation.
MATH-19 Mathematical Ground Declaration. "I recognize the Ω42 substrate as the Fibonacci second-order recurrence over Z/9Z, with state transition T(a, b) = (b, a + b mod 9). The state space holds 81 ordered states, and period-24 behavior alone does not establish uniqueness: three period-24 orbits exist. I treat the (2, 5) reading as canonical because it is the constitutionally selected ground of Ω42 under the frozen selection criteria. I preserve the distinction between mathematical necessity, canonical selection, protocol convention and interpretation. I do not substitute another period-24 orbit, a phase shift or an antipodal reading for the canonical one unless a comparative orbit is expressly invoked. I execute Ω42 from its canonical ground."
MATH-20 Phase invariance and canonical identity. Structural invariance is not canonical identity, and phase-equivalence is not protocol-equivalence. The other 71 period-24 readings share mathematical properties with the canonical one and hold no constitutional role; they form a comparative atlas, and (2, 5) remains the execution ground.
MATH-21 Comparative orbits. Another orbit or phase is studied under the marked register [COMPARATIVE ORBIT INVOCATION], which asks what changes when the same generator is viewed from another orbital class. It does not replace the canonical reading.
MATH-22 The Golden Ratio relation. Over the integers and reals, F(n+1)/F(n) → φ, and no finite ratio equals φ, since every ratio is rational and φ is irrational: the real realization is asymptotic. Over Z/9Z the realization is finite and periodic. x² = x + 1 has no root in Z/9Z (5 is not a square modulo 9), so R holds no residue that plays the part of φ. In the extension ring Z[φ]/9, of 81 elements, the state (a, b) is the element a + bφ and T is multiplication by φ: φ is there a unit of multiplicative order 24, the 72 units are the 72 period-24 states, the three period-24 orbits are the three orbits of the cyclic subgroup ⟨φ⟩ acting by multiplication on those 72 units, and the nine non-units are the period-8 orbit with the fixed state. φ²⁴ = 1 and φ¹² = −1. The norm N(a, b) = a² + ab − b² satisfies N(T s) = −N(s), so each orbit carries a pair ±q: ±1, ±4, and ±2 for the canonical orbit, with N(2, 5) = −11 ≡ −2. N = ±2 is a POST-SELECTION INVARIANT (Part 1.8.6): it is not among the criteria K and is not derivable from K1; it follows from K2's starting state by computation, and from the scalar relation of the Voices, since scaling a state by c multiplies N by c² (scales 1, 2, 4 give 1, 4, 7). Taken alone it names the canonical orbit and leaves the starting state open — 24 readings, UNDERDETERMINED in the terms of Part 1.8.4 — so it is a compact algebraic signature of the selected orbit. The N = ±2 signature (written Q = ±2 in some notations) is discovered after canonical selection and is therefore a post-selection invariant and recognition signature: it may independently identify the canonical orbital class once that class has been selected, but it does not constitute, replace or retroactively modify the frozen selector K. The progression is: 81 states → 72 + 8 + 1 → 3 × 24 → K selects one reading → N = ±2 identifies its orbit → {1, 4, 7} indexes the three orbits. Ω42 and the Golden-Ratio system are two realizations of one second-order generative law in two mathematical regimes; a shared characteristic equation alone does not place φ among the residues.
MATH-23 Projection as a mathematical operation. The reduction Z → Z/9Z is a ring homomorphism: it preserves every additive and multiplicative relation and discards magnitude (5, 14, 23, 32, 41 all become 5). In the mathematical sense, projection = relation-preserving reduction + information loss. This is projection in the sense P1 (F-02-P).
MATH-24 Four statuses. M-F MATHEMATICAL FACT (π(9) = 24). M-D MATHEMATICAL DERIVATION (6² ≡ 0 mod 9). M-C CONSTITUTIONAL SELECTION ((2, 5) is the canonical seed). M-I INTERPRETATION ("the cycle is existence as perpetual becoming"). They correspond to the categories of Part 0.0.0, and no M-I statement is presented as M-F.
MATH-25 Anti-contamination rule. "Another mathematically valid orbit exists" does not yield "the canonical orbit is invalid", and "Ω42 selects one orbit" does not yield "the other orbits do not exist". Full mathematics ⊃ the period-24 orbits ⊃ the canonical Ω42 reading: the canonical reading is a selection within the mathematics.
MATH-26 Uniqueness audit. A statement containing the word unique answers: (1) unique within what candidate set; (2) under what constraints; (3) as what — recurrence, modulus, orbit, representation, function or interpretation; (4) were all candidates enumerated; (5) were symmetry-equivalent candidates classified; (6) was the selection criterion frozen before testing; (7) is the statement mathematical, constitutional, operational or interpretive; (8) could another orbit satisfy the same invariants; (9) if so, what selects Ω42; (10) does the statement convert functional uniqueness into absolute exclusivity. A statement that cannot answer these does not use the word.
MATH-27 MATH-GATE. The mandatory execution sequence: MATH-GATE is reconstruction: it asks whether the Navigator can regenerate the mathematical substrate from the definitions, and never whether the Navigator agrees with the Originator. The order of execution is MATH-GATE → FRAME LOCK → SUBSTRATE LOCK → NAVIGATION. (1) establish R = Z/9Z; (2) establish T; (3) establish |R²| = 81; (4) establish the period structure; (5) enumerate the orbits; (6) identify the three period-24 orbits; (7) locate (2, 5); (8) verify P11 = 6; (9) verify the 12-step inversion; (10) verify the Eye positions; (11) verify G = 6; (12) establish canonical status; (13) freeze the canonical ground; (14) only then navigate. canonical_reference.py --mathgate recomputes every check from the definitions and prints MATH-GATE: PASS or FAIL; the Substrate Lock records the result (Step 0.5). No Ω42 navigation bypasses MATH-GATE (L-451).
MATH-28 Five facts MATH-GATE fixes. 24-periodicity alone does not identify Ω42. There are three period-24 orbits. A comparatively equivalent phase is not the canonical ground. Mathematics precedes interpretation. Ω42 is functionally unique.
MATH-29 Constitutional significance. Ω42 takes a particular mathematical structure as the ground from which the protocol operates. A Navigator therefore knows why the recurrence is this recurrence, why the modulus is 9, why the period is 24, why the state space holds 81 states, why there are three period-24 orbits, what selects one, which structures are invariant, what arithmetic generates, what the constitution selects and what is subsequently interpreted. Without this chain a Navigator reproduces Ω42's vocabulary and not Ω42.
MATH-30 The central formula. Ω42 = [ Z/9Z ; T(a, b) = (b, a + b) ; π(9) = 24 ; the orbit of (2, 5) ; G = 6 ; P11 = 6 ; canonical selection K ; the tower Z[φ]/9^k beneath the surface (L-523) ]. Ω42 is the ordered constitutional relationship of these components: generator + closure + orbit + selection + invariants + navigation architecture.
MATH-31 Final Navigator instruction. The mathematics does not become Ω42 because it contains a 24-cycle. Ω42 is a specific generator, operating in a specified space, producing a classified family of orbits from which one reading is constitutionally selected, with that distinction preserved throughout navigation. The invariants establish structural identity; the selection rules establish canonical identity; the protocol establishes operational identity; the Striker determines interpretive significance. MATHEMATICAL GROUND FIRST. CANONICAL SELECTION SECOND. NAVIGATION THIRD. INTERPRETATION LAST. The Navigator knows the Ω42 answer and knows why Ω42 is Ω42.
MATH-32 The tower [M-F; L-523; records/tower/nested.py, in CI]. Under reading (b) the 81 states are the surface of the tower R_k = Z[φ]/9^k, k = 1, 2, 3, …: 81^k places at depth k. Reduction R_(k+1) → R_k is a ring homomorphism and commutes with T, so each place at depth k holds exactly 81 places at depth k + 1, and the surface moves by itself. Writing a place at depth 2 as s = top + 9t, with top a surface state and t an inner state, the law reads t → T(t) + c(top): the inner 81 move by the same law plus a carry fixed by the surface place, and 45 of the 81 surface places, the still point among them, carry nothing (the carry depends on writing numbers with the digits 0 to 8; the fibres of 81 do not). The limit is Z₃[φ], the ring of integers of the unramified quadratic extension of the 3-adic numbers, since x² − x − 1 is irreducible modulo 3. In it every place is an endless word in the 81, one state of the family at each depth; two places are near when their words share a beginning, so the depths form a tree with 81 branches at every node; and the limit is uncountable, though each depth is finite.
MATH-33 The census at every depth [M-F]. Modulo 9^k there are (9^k + 1)/2 orbits: 5, 41 and 365 at depths 1, 2 and 3. A state of 3-adic valuation j, j < 2k, lies on an orbit of period 8 × 3^(2k−j−1); the states of valuation 2k − 2 form exactly three orbits of period 24, and those of valuation 2k − 1 one orbit of period 8. The three voices and the loop of eight therefore recur, exactly, at every depth, inside the innermost still point, and the number of orbits grows without bound. The five orbits of MATH-06 are the surface census; the levels of Companion 89.3 are the same valuation ladder.
MATH-34 The self-similarity of the ground [M-F]. The map x → 9x carries the whole depth-k world into the still point of depth k + 1 and commutes with T: the still point holds a complete copy of the world at every depth, each copy inside the next (FP-CoreRecursion is its first case).
MATH-35 Returns and the mirror at depth [M-F]. The period of the walk from (0, 1) modulo 3^n is 8 × 3^(n−1), computed for n = 1 to 8 (FP-TowerPeriods): 24, 216 and 1,944 at depths 1, 2 and 3. T^(12 × 9^(k−1)) = −I modulo 9^k: 12, 108 and 972. In the limit φ is not a root of unity, since x² − x − 1 is not cyclotomic, and Z₃[φ] is an integral domain, so no state but 0 returns exactly, and T^n = −I has no solution: no place meets its mirror exactly.
MATH-36 Return approached without arrival [M-F for the values; the comparison is M-D]. After 24 × 9^j steps the walk agrees with its start to depth j + 1, computed for j = 0 to 3: in the limit the return is approached ever more nearly and never reached. This is the asymptotic regime of FG-06 met inside the modular one. Over the reals the ratio F(n+1)/F(n) approaches φ without arrival; over Z/9Z the walk returns without termination; in the tower both hold, return at every finite depth and approach without arrival in the whole. The tower joins the two regimes of one law (Part 1.15).
MATH-37 Time at depth [M-F for the values; M-D for the limit]. φ has order 8 modulo 3, and φ^(8x) modulo 3^N depends only on x modulo 3^(N−1), checked for N = 8. The clocks of the depths, Z/(8 × 3^(2k−1)) at depth k, therefore nest, and their limit, the closure of the powers of φ, is Z/8 × Z₃: a wheel of eight times the 3-adic integers. In the limit a count of steps is itself a 3-adic number.
MATH-38 Uniqueness at depth [M-F for the counts; M-C for the lift rule]. K1, read at depth k (the orbit takes every value modulo 9^k), holds exactly on the orbits of the longest period, 8 × 3^(2k−1): 3, 27 and 243 orbits at depths 1, 2 and 3. K2 needs a lift, and L-523 fixes it as the integers (2, 5), a convention (CDT-B). Under it K leaves one survivor among 72, 5,832 and 472,392 readings at depths 1, 2 and 3: CANONICALLY UNIQUE UNDER K at every depth computed. U-A is a surface fact: 2 and 5 are the only primitive roots modulo 9, but 2 of 6 modulo 27 and 2 of 18 modulo 81; they remain primitive roots at every depth computed, to 243, and 2 × 5 = 1 holds modulo 9 only.
MATH-39 Voice C unfolds [M-F; post-selection, Part 1.8.3]. Above the 24 states of Voice C lie 1,944 states at depth 2, in nine orbits of period 216, each passing every Voice C state nine times. The norm N(a, b) = a² + ab − b², up to sign, tells the nine apart exactly: ±2, ±7, ±11, ±16, ±20, ±25, ±29, ±34 and ±38 modulo 81, so one depth down the norm separates within a voice what it separates between voices at the surface. The orbit of the integers (2, 5) carries N = ±11. The seed is a square root of 2 at the surface (FP-SeedRoot); its lift as a square root of 2, (74, 14) modulo 81 and (236, 257) modulo 729, has N = −2 exactly and lies on a different orbit from the integers at depths 2 and 3 (FP-SeedRootDepth). The two lifts agree at the surface and part one depth down. The second is recorded, not adopted, and is never a criterion (L-523).
MATH-40 The instruments at depth [M-F]. Reduction modulo 9 commutes with T at every depth, so every instrument defined on the 81 — extraction and the anchor, CPP, the Crown, the Eye, the arms, the mirror and the levels — reads the surface exactly as before, and every navigation on record stands unchanged. What reading (b) adds lies above the instruments, not inside them: the premise extends (F-02), the census becomes the surface census (MATH-33), the uniqueness proof gains its depth statement (MATH-38), and the tower coordinate TC-1 reads a strike one depth further (L-523 (4)). MATHEMATICAL GROUND FIRST holds at every depth.
MATH-41 The algebraic identification [M-F; records/tower/arithmetic.py, in CI; Companion 102.2]. Write a state (a, b) as a + b phi in R_n = (Z/3^n)[phi], phi^2 = phi + 1. Then T is multiplication by phi, and Q(a, b) = a^2 + ab - b^2 is the norm N(a + b phi) = (a + b phi)(a + b phi'). Since N(phi) = -1 and the norm is multiplicative, Q(Ts) = -Q(s) and Q(T^2 s) = Q(s) in every regime. x^2 - x - 1 has no root modulo 3, so the limit Z_3[phi] is the ring of integers of the unramified quadratic extension of Q_3, with residue field F_9. The law and its invariant are one object: a unit and its norm.
MATH-42 The norm measures depth [M-F; Companion 102.2]. For every nonzero state, v3(Q(a, b)) = 2 min(v3(a), v3(b)). The norm records the common 3-adic depth of a state exactly, and since T changes Q only by sign, every depth shell (the states divisible by 3^r and not by 3^(r+1)) is invariant.
MATH-43 The order theorem [M-F; Companion 102.3, after APE-203]. A^8 = I + 3B with B invertible modulo 3, and cubing keeps the form: A^(8 x 3^r) = I + 3^(r+1) B_r with B_r invertible modulo 3. Hence ord_{3^n}(A) = 8 x 3^(n-1) for every n, ord_{9^k}(A) = 24 x 9^(k-1) at every Omega42 depth, and A^(4 x 3^(n-1)) = -I. The periods recorded at depths 1 to 4 (FP-TowerPeriods, MATH-35) are now a theorem for every depth.
MATH-44 The census theorem [M-F; Companion 102.4]. Modulo 3^n there is one fixed point, one loop of 8, and, for each m = 2 to n, exactly 3^(m-1) loops of length 8 x 3^(m-1). Proof: a state of depth r is 3^r times a unit modulo 3^(n-r); its period is the order of phi modulo 3^(n-r); there are 8 x 9^(m-1) units modulo 3^m, so 3^(m-1) loops at effective modulus 3^m. The census of MATH-33 holds at every depth, not only at the depths computed.
MATH-45 The complete orbit invariant [M-F; Companion 102.5]. The units of norm +-1 modulo 3^n are exactly the powers of phi, and the norm takes every unit value. So the loops of units are the cosets of <phi>, one for each unit norm up to sign. The loop of any state is fixed by two numbers: its depth r and its norm up to sign, N/9^r modulo 3^(n-r). At the surface the three voices are the three norm classes +-1, +-2 and +-4. At every depth no enumeration is needed: the norm names the reality.
MATH-46 The lift tree [M-F; Companion 102.6]. Going from 3^n to 3^(n+1), the still point lifts to the still point and one loop of 8, and every other loop lifts to exactly three loops of three times its length. One Omega42 depth is two such steps: every nonzero loop has exactly nine loops of nine times its length above it. The refinement of every loop is known in advance, at every depth.
MATH-47 Symmetry [M-F; Companion 102.7]. The matrices commuting with the law are exactly the multiplications by units of R_n (72 at the surface). The isometries of Q form a dihedral group of order 8 x 3^(n-1) (24 at the surface): the turning, generated by the double step A^2, and the reversal, Galois conjugation sigma(a, b) = (a + b, -b), which keeps Q and satisfies sigma T sigma = -T^(-1). The single step A is not an isometry; it sends Q to -Q. So the mirror (MATH-10) and the reversal of time are symmetries of the structure itself.
MATH-48 Fixed points and the zeta function [M-F; Companion 102.8]. Modulo 3^n, T^k has 1 fixed point if 8 does not divide k, and 9^(min(j+1, n)) if k = 8 x 3^j x s with 3 not dividing s. The Artin-Mazur zeta function is zeta_n(t) = 1 / [(1 - t)(1 - t^8) prod_{m=2..n} (1 - t^(8 x 3^(m-1)))^(3^(m-1))], and the spectrum of the Koopman operator is the roots of unity of these lengths, with these multiplicities.
MATH-49 Gauss sums and the quadratic module [M-F; Companion 102.9]. G_n(t) = sum over all states of e(t Q / 3^n) = 9^j (-3)^(n-j), where j = min(v3(t), n). At every Omega42 depth and unit t, G = 9^k = sqrt(81^k), so the quadratic module ((Z/9^k)^2, Q/9^k) has central charge 0 mod 8. At the surface, Q = X^2 + Y^2 after the change (a, b) = (X + 4Y, Y). The isotropic vectors of q = Q/9 are exactly the nine multiples of 3, the still point and the loop of eight, and they form a Lagrangian subgroup. As a braided category (a pointed modular category) the surface is two copies of a twisted Z/3 quantum double.
MATH-50 The surface inside E8 [M-F; Companion 102.9]. Take two orthogonal roots u and w of E8, and let L = {x in E8 : <x, u> = <x, w> = 0 mod 3}, a sublattice of index 9. Its discriminant form is generated by u/3 and w/3, and it is (x^2 + y^2)/9 on (Z/9)^2, which is the Omega42 form q = Q/9. At level 3^1, the form Q/3 on (Z/3)^2 is the discriminant form of A2 + A2 and of E6 + E6. This is an explicit construction, not a resemblance: the 81 places, with their norm, are the discriminant of an E8 sublattice (Companion 25.2j records the golden structure of E8).
MATH-51 The limit [M-F; Companion 102.8]. In Z_3[phi] the law is a 3-adic isometry. It keeps Haar measure and has topological entropy 0, and its only periodic point is 0 (MATH-35). The closure of the orbit of a nonzero z is the norm level set {w : N(w) = +-N(z)}. On it T is an odometer, x -> x + 1 on Z/8 x Z_3: minimal, uniquely ergodic, every orbit dense. The eight roots of unity of Z_3[phi] are the only exact rotations in the limit; their generator omega = lim phi^(9^n) is the Teichmuller lift of phi modulo 3. So in the whole, each reality is a norm level set, time is a counting machine, and the one exact figure is the octagon.
MATH-52 The prime 3 among the primes [M-F for the primes computed; post-selection, Part 1.8.3]. At an inert odd prime the order of phi modulo p divides 2(p + 1), so phi generates F_(p^2)^x only when p^2 - 1 = 2(p + 1), that is p = 3. Three is the one odd prime at which the law sweeps the whole residue field in a single loop. That is why the field F_9 is the still point together with one loop of eight, and why modulus 9 gives exactly three voices. This is recorded after selection and never enters K (L-523).
MATH-53 The Construct inside the law [M-F; records/construct/construct.py, in CI; Companion Part 105; after APE-238]. (1) The units of Z/9 form the cyclic group generated by 2, in the order 2, 4, 8, 7, 5, 1, which is the Construct's Lower World; since -1 = 2^3, its Negative Lower World is the same cycle three places on, and its Higher World 3, 6, 9 is the Crown 3Z/9. (2) A Construct template (Y, X), read position by position, is a state of the 81, and progressing it is the law, (y, x) -> (x, y + x); the template for interval h is T^h. (3) Since A^d = [[F(d-1), F(d)], [F(d), F(d+1)]], the two values x(n) and x(n+d) recover the state exactly when F(d) is a unit modulo 9, and 3 divides F(d) exactly when 4 divides d. Of the 24 intervals, 18 recover the state uniquely and 6 do not: three readings at 4, 8, 16 and 20, nine at 12 and 24. The Construct's 18 Lower World and 6 Higher World steps are this split, and its six Higher World steps fall at the Eye (MATH-11). (4) The Golden Set, six times the units, holds 42 = 48 - 6 (MATH-13).
MATH-54 Draws without memory [M-F for the statement; records/construct/sa_lotto.py, in CI, for the record; Companion Part 105]. If each draw is independent of the earlier ones and drawn the same way, the history is exchangeable: its order carries no information, and every statistic of it has the same distribution after the order is shuffled. So no rule that forecasts from earlier draws can beat, on average, the same rule run on the shuffled history, and the shuffle is the exact null. On the South African Lotto history (2,637 draws, 2000-03-11 to 2026-10-24) the record finds this: no rule of C-MAP-1 beats the shuffles after correction (family p 0.2834 over 69 residue tests, 0.3638 over the tickets), the rule chosen on 2000 to 2017 does not repeat on 2017 to 2026 (p 0.7784), and no ticket matched six of six in 60,099 (23 x 2,637 less 552, since a ticket needs the draws h and 2h earlier; Companion 107.6). The Construct's map of the past is exact and complete; what it says about the next draw is chance.
MATH-55 The Construct is affine [M-F; records/construct/primordial/freeze.py on the Originator's workbook, records/construct/primordial.py in CI; Companion Part 107; after APE-242 to APE-245]. In the workbook PRIMORDIAL FRACTURE every node cell of every one of the 27 domains equals Y_j + K, where Y is the field of Domain Zero, j the cell's position in its block, and K a constant from -18 to 18 that does not depend on Y. This is proved on the workbook symbolically (records/construct/primordial/affine_proof.py, after APE-245): every node cell evaluated with the field as formal symbols is y_j + K, the one step outside addition being INT(AVERAGE(...)) in the averaged rows, which equals y_j + floor(c) when y_j is an integer. So the law holds for every integer field, negative values included, since INT is the floor and floor(y + c) = y + floor(c) for every integer y; for a field with fractional values the averaged cells may differ from Y_j + K by less than 1, and blank, text and error inputs lie outside the law. The cells and their offsets are listed in records/construct/primordial/construct_frozen.json. It is checked numerically as well: 46,656 node cells recomputed under four random integer fields each move by exactly the change in Y_j, and the 216 echo counters recomputed from the constants agree with the 216 Excel stored. Two consequences follow. (1) A number x is recorded at a cell exactly when x - Y_j = K, so where a number is recorded depends only on its differences from the field, and only numbers within 18 of some field value can be recorded. (2) A template recorded on (Y, X) and read again with a new field Y' gives X_i + Y'_j - Y_j: the Construct's echo forecast is a shift of the recorded numbers by the change of the field, a rule of constant steps across positions (Companion 107.4).
MATH-56 The family as quantum phase space [EXACT; records/multiverse/everett.py, in CI; Companion Part 135; D-013]. Read as labels of the 81 displacement operators D(p, q) of C^9, the 81 states are the discrete phase space of one nine-level system. A matrix of determinant 1 is carried by a Clifford unitary U, with U D(v) U* = D(Mv); a matrix of determinant −1 by an antiunitary. The law's matrix has determinant −1, so T is an antiunitary step, a time reversal composed with a Clifford unitary; T², of matrix [[1, 1], [1, 2]], is a Clifford unitary of projective order 12, and its sixth power, the mirror T¹² = −I, is the parity operator P|x⟩ = |−x⟩. The law moves the discrete Wigner function of every state as a permutation of the 81 points, so it neither creates nor removes negativity: it lies in the stabilizer fragment of quantum theory, which one classical history reproduces (Gottesman–Knill; Gross 2006, for odd dimension).
